Oh, well... I wouldn't say I strictly limit things, no. I mean, I try to be good, of course. I think we all try to watch what we eat, especially at my age when the metabolism slows down, right?

Oh, dear... um... well, it's usually only if I feel I've really overdone things. I certainly wouldn't do that after just a regular, sensible meal. That wouldn't be right at all.

It's so embarrassing to admit, but I might have some leftover casserole, and then some of the cookies I baked, and crackers, and... well, some ice cream, all sort of in one sitting. It's much, much more than I’d ever put on a plate if anyone else were there to see it, of course! \n\nI know it's a silly thing to do at my age. But it's really only after something like that, when I've eaten too much. Does that make sense? I hope you don't think terribly of me for it, I just try to fix the mistake so things stay manageable.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up to undo the calories from the extra food you eat during those times when you feel you've overeaten?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... yes. Yes, I suppose that is exactly it. I feel so terribly guilty about all those extra calories, and I just... I don't want to gain weight after being so foolish. \n\nTo be perfectly honest with you, Doctor—and it is so hard to say this out loud—that's actually what finally scared me into coming in to see you. After I eat too much, I... well, I've been making myself throw up in the bathroom so I won't gain the weight. I kept telling myself I could just do it once in a while to fix a bad evening, but... it’s gotten to where it isn’t just a one-time mistake anymore. \n\nI know it sounds awful for a woman my age to be doing such a thing. You must think I'm very silly, don't you? I just want to keep things under control and do what's right. I hope you can understand... am I making any sense at all?\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up a way for you to feel like you've undone the eating?\n\nPatient: Oh... yes, exactly. I'm sorry, I know I just said that, but... yes. It's... it's just my way of trying to fix it, to undo what I just did. \n\nLike I mentioned, that's really what brought me here today. It's just so frightening to admit, but after I have one of those... those episodes where I eat too much, I go into the bathroom and I make myself sick. I just get so panicked about gaining weight from all that food. \n\nI kept thinking, \"Oh, I'll just do this one time, just to undo this one mistake,\" but... well, it's not just a one-time thing anymore, is it? It's become a bit of a habit, I'm afraid. I know I'm repeating myself, I'm just so nervous talking about it. I hope you aren't too disappointed in me. I really am trying to do the right thing by telling you, aren't I?\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you feel that purging is necessary to maintain a body shape or weight that you consider acceptable?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 66 / retail_salesperson / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance\nmanifestation: \"If my skirt feels tight before church or I catch my side view in the store mirror, it can ruin my whole day because I feel like everyone can tell I’ve let myself go.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, dear... well, yes. I suppose it is. I mean, I try to keep myself looking presentable, you know? It's important to take pride in your appearance, isn't it? 

But... well, I do get terribly upset about it sometimes. More than I probably should. If I'm getting dressed for church on Sunday and my skirt feels even a little bit snug... or if I'm at the store and I catch a glimpse of my side view in one of the mirrors... oh, it just ruins my whole day. I start to feel so awful, like... like I've completely let myself go, and I just know everyone else can see it too. 

It's so silly to care so much at 66, I know. A woman my age should be past all this fussing. But I just want to look nice and acceptable, and when I feel heavy, I just... I panic. That's why I end up doing what I do, just to try and keep things where they should be. I hope that doesn't sound too vain or foolish to you? I really do try my best to be sensible most of the time.

I kept telling myself I could just do it once in a while to fix a bad evening, but... it’s gotten to where it isn’t just a one-time mistake anymore. \n\nI know it sounds awful for a woman my age to be doing such a thing. You must think I'm very silly, don't you? I just want to keep things under control and do what's right. I hope you can understand... am I making any sense at all?\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up a way for you to feel like you've undone the eating?\n\nPatient: Oh... yes, exactly. I'm sorry, I know I just said that, but... yes. It's... it's just my way of trying to fix it, to undo what I just did. \n\nLike I mentioned, that's really what brought me here today. It's just so frightening to admit, but after I have one of those... those episodes where I eat too much, I go into the bathroom and I make myself sick. I just get so panicked about gaining weight from all that food. \n\nI kept thinking, \"Oh, I'll just do this one time, just to undo this one mistake,\" but... well, it's not just a one-time thing anymore, is it? It's become a bit of a habit, I'm afraid. I know I'm repeating myself, I'm just so nervous talking about it. I hope you aren't too disappointed in me. I really am trying to do the right thing by telling you, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that purging is necessary to maintain a body shape or weight that you consider acceptable?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... well, yes. I suppose it is. I mean, I try to keep myself looking presentable, you know? It's important to take pride in your appearance, isn't it? \n\nBut... well, I do get terribly upset about it sometimes. More than I probably should. If I'm getting dressed for church on Sunday and my skirt feels even a little bit snug... or if I'm at the store and I catch a glimpse of my side view in one of the mirrors... oh, it just ruins my whole day. I start to feel so awful, like... like I've completely let myself go, and I just know everyone else can see it too. \n\nIt's so silly to care so much at 66, I know. A woman my age should be past all this fussing. But I just want to look nice and acceptable, and when I feel heavy, I just... I panic. That's why I end up doing what I do, just to try and keep things where they should be. I hope that doesn't sound too vain or foolish to you? I really do try my best to be sensible most of the time.\n\n[Last Question]\nIs making yourself throw up only after eating a lot more than usual a way to counteract the calories from that extra food?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. I'd hate for you to think I'm completely obsessed or making a fuss over nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are overweight even when you are at a normal weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... no, I don't think so. I mean, I know what the scale says, and I know what size clothes I wear. I don't think I'm seeing things that aren't there. \n\nIf my doctor tells me my weight is in a healthy range, I believe them. I just... I suppose I just prefer to stay on the lower end of that range, if I can. It just feels a bit more comfortable, you know? I don't think I have a distorted view of myself or anything like that. I just like to look neat and tidy. That's a perfectly reasonable way to feel, don't you think? I certainly wouldn't want to seem irrational about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I haven't really noticed any big changes. It stays pretty much the same, honestly. \n\nI mean, maybe a pound or two here and there depending on the week, just like anyone else, but nothing significant at all. Being on my feet all day at the store helps keep things pretty steady, I think. I just try to maintain a nice, even weight so my clothes fit right. \n\nThat's what you doctors usually prefer, right? No big ups and downs? I hope I'm doing that part right, at least. I really do try to keep it all very manageable.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually make yourself throw up after eating a normal amount of food or only after eating a lot more than usual?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... um... well, it's usually only if I feel I've really overdone things. I certainly wouldn't do that after just a regular, sensible meal. That wouldn't be right at all. \n\nBut... well, sometimes, if I've had a late shift at the store, or maybe after a bridge night... I might come home and just... eat a bit more than I meant to. It's so embarrassing to admit, but I might have some leftover casserole, and then some of the cookies I baked, and crackers, and... well, some ice cream, all sort of in one sitting. It's much, much more than I’d ever put on a plate if anyone else were there to see it, of course! \n\nI know it's a silly thing to do at my age. But it's really only after something like that, when I've eaten too much. Does that make sense? I hope you don't think terribly of me for it, I just try to fix the mistake so things stay manageable.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up to undo the calories from the extra food you eat during those times when you feel you've overeaten?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... yes. I don't want to gain weight after being so foolish. \n\nTo be perfectly honest with you, Doctor—and it is so hard to say this out loud—that's actually what finally scared me into coming in to see you. After I eat too much, I... well, I've been making myself throw up in the bathroom so I won't gain the weight. I kept telling myself I could just do it once in a while to fix a bad evening, but... it’s gotten to where it isn’t just a one-time mistake anymore. \n\nI know it sounds awful for a woman my age to be doing such a thing. I just want to do whatever I need to do to get this sorted out.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up regularly as part of your normal routine to control your weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... the word \"routine\" sounds so dreadful, doesn't it? I never, ever wanted it to be a normal part of my life. But... well, if I'm being completely truthful with you—and I really am trying to be a good patient and tell you everything—I suppose it has become exactly that. \n\nFor at least the past several months now, it’s... well, it's been happening most weeks. I'll have one of those awful secret eating spells we talked about, and then I just... I go and make myself sick afterward. I kept telling myself it was just every now and then, but I can’t honestly call it occasional anymore, as much as I wish I could. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that's probably very bad to hear. I hope you aren't too disappointed in me for letting it get to this point? I really am trying my best to fix it by being honest with you today.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the past year where you lost a significant amount of weight, only to regain it later?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no. No, I haven't really had anything like that. My weight has stayed pretty much the same, give or take a pound or two here and there. I haven't had any big drops or anything dramatic like that. \n\nI try to keep things steady, you see. I hope that's a good thing? I mean, I know the... the other things I've told you aren't good at all, but at least my weight hasn't been bouncing all over the place. That's a little bit of a positive, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you usually make yourself throw up only after eating episodes where you feel like you've lost control?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 66 / retail_salesperson / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, goodness... I'm actually not entirely sure. I haven't really noticed if it's *only* when I feel like I've completely lost control. 

I mean, I know it definitely happens after those... those bigger eating spells we just talked about. But as for whether it's strictly tied to feeling a complete loss of control every single time... I just haven't paid close enough attention to my exact feelings in the moment to say for certain. 

I'm so sorry, I feel like I should have a much better answer for you! I hope I'm not being unhelpful or making your job harder. I could certainly start keeping a little notebook to track exactly how I'm feeling right before it happens, if you think that would be a good idea? I really just want to make sure I'm doing this right.

I try so hard not to be vain, I really do. But... well, if I'm getting ready for church and my skirt feels even a little bit tight, or... or if I catch a glimpse of my side view in one of the mirrors at the store where I work... it just, oh, it can completely ruin my whole day. \n\nI just get this awful feeling that everyone is looking at me and can tell I've completely let myself go. I know that sounds so foolish for a woman my age to fret over, doesn't it? I really hope you don't think I'm just being terribly shallow.\n\nDoctor: Do you often limit the amount or type of food you eat to avoid gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I wouldn't say I strictly limit things, no. I mean, I try to be good, of course. I think we all try to watch what we eat, especially at my age when the metabolism slows down, right? \n\nI might, um... I might skip a dessert or try to have a salad instead of a sandwich for lunch, just to be sensible. But I don't do those extreme diets or starve myself or anything like that. I try to eat normally most of the time. I just... I try to make healthy choices so I don't get too heavy. That's a normal thing to do, isn't it? I hope that's the right way to handle it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is a problem even when others tell you that you look fine?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I'm not really sure I'd say that. To be perfectly honest, people don't usually comment on my weight one way or the other these days. My friends are very sweet, of course, but we just don't really talk about our bodies much at our age. \n\nAnd I certainly wouldn't ever argue with someone if they were kind enough to tell me I looked nice! I'd just say thank you. I don't think I'm... you know, imagining things or being completely unreasonable when I feel a bit heavy. I just want to look presentable for work and for church, really. I hope that sounds like a normal way to look at things? I'd hate for you to think I'm completely obsessed or making a fuss over nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are overweight even when you are at a normal weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... no, I don't think so. I mean, I know what the scale says, and I know what size clothes I wear. I don't think I'm seeing things that aren't there. \n\nIf my doctor tells me my weight is in a healthy range, I believe them. I just... I suppose I just prefer to stay on the lower end of that range, if I can. It just feels a bit more comfortable, you know? I don't think I have a distorted view of myself or anything like that. I just like to look neat and tidy. That's a perfectly reasonable way to feel, don't you think? I certainly wouldn't want to seem irrational about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I haven't really noticed any big changes. It stays pretty much the same, honestly. \n\nI mean, maybe a pound or two here and there depending on the week, just like anyone else, but nothing significant at all. Being on my feet all day at the store helps keep things pretty steady, I think. I just try to maintain a nice, even weight so my clothes fit right. \n\nThat's what you doctors usually prefer, right? No big ups and downs? I hope I'm doing that part right, at least. I really do try to keep it all very manageable.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually make yourself throw up after eating a normal amount of food or only after eating a lot more than usual?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... um... well, it's usually only if I feel I've really overdone things. I certainly wouldn't do that after just a regular, sensible meal. That wouldn't be right at all. \n\nBut... well, sometimes, if I've had a late shift at the store, or maybe after a bridge night... I might come home and just... eat a bit more than I meant to. It's so embarrassing to admit, but I might have some leftover casserole, and then some of the cookies I baked, and crackers, and... well, some ice cream, all sort of in one sitting. It's much, much more than I’d ever put on a plate if anyone else were there to see it, of course! \n\nI know it's a silly thing to do at my age. But it's really only after something like that, when I've eaten too much. Does that make sense? I hope you don't think terribly of me for it, I just try to fix the mistake so things stay manageable.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up to undo the calories from the extra food you eat during those times when you feel you've overeaten?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... yes. Yes, I suppose that is exactly it. I feel so terribly guilty about all those extra calories, and I just... I don't want to gain weight after being so foolish. \n\nTo be perfectly honest with you, Doctor—and it is so hard to say this out loud—that's actually what finally scared me into coming in to see you. After I eat too much, I... well, I've been making myself throw up in the bathroom so I won't gain the weight. I kept telling myself I could just do it once in a while to fix a bad evening, but... it’s gotten to where it isn’t just a one-time mistake anymore. \n\nI know it sounds awful for a woman my age to be doing such a thing. You must think I'm very silly, don't you? I just want to keep things under control and do what's right. I hope you can understand... am I making any sense at all?\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up a way for you to feel like you've undone the eating?\n\nPatient: Oh... yes, exactly. I'm sorry, I know I just said that, but... yes. It's... it's just my way of trying to fix it, to undo what I just did. \n\nLike I mentioned, that's really what brought me here today. It's just so frightening to admit, but after I have one of those... those episodes where I eat too much, I go into the bathroom and I make myself sick. I just get so panicked about gaining weight from all that food. \n\nI kept thinking, \"Oh, I'll just do this one time, just to undo this one mistake,\" but... well, it's not just a one-time thing anymore, is it? I really am trying to do the right thing by telling you, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that purging is necessary to maintain a body shape or weight that you consider acceptable?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... well, yes. I think we all try to watch what we eat, especially at my age when the metabolism slows down, right? \n\nI might, um... I might skip a dessert or try to have a salad instead of a sandwich for lunch, just to be sensible. But I don't do those extreme diets or starve myself or anything like that. I try to eat normally most of the time. I just... I try to make healthy choices so I don't get too heavy. That's a normal thing to do, isn't it? I hope that's the right way to handle it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is a problem even when others tell you that you look fine?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I'm not really sure I'd say that. To be perfectly honest, people don't usually comment on my weight one way or the other these days. My friends are very sweet, of course, but we just don't really talk about our bodies much at our age. \n\nAnd I certainly wouldn't ever argue with someone if they were kind enough to tell me I looked nice! I'd just say thank you. I don't think I'm... you know, imagining things or being completely unreasonable when I feel a bit heavy. I just want to look presentable for work and for church, really. I hope that sounds like a normal way to look at things? I'd hate for you to think I'm completely obsessed or making a fuss over nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are overweight even when you are at a normal weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... no, I don't think so. I mean, I know what the scale says, and I know what size clothes I wear. I don't think I'm seeing things that aren't there. \n\nIf my doctor tells me my weight is in a healthy range, I believe them. I just... I suppose I just prefer to stay on the lower end of that range, if I can. It just feels a bit more comfortable, you know? I don't think I have a distorted view of myself or anything like that. I just like to look neat and tidy. That's a perfectly reasonable way to feel, don't you think? I certainly wouldn't want to seem irrational about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I haven't really noticed any big changes. It stays pretty much the same, honestly. \n\nI mean, maybe a pound or two here and there depending on the week, just like anyone else, but nothing significant at all. Being on my feet all day at the store helps keep things pretty steady, I think. I just try to maintain a nice, even weight so my clothes fit right. \n\nThat's what you doctors usually prefer, right? No big ups and downs? I hope I'm doing that part right, at least. I really do try to keep it all very manageable.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually make yourself throw up after eating a normal amount of food or only after eating a lot more than usual?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... um... well, it's usually only if I feel I've really overdone things. I certainly wouldn't do that after just a regular, sensible meal. That wouldn't be right at all. \n\nBut... well, sometimes, if I've had a late shift at the store, or maybe after a bridge night... I might come home and just... eat a bit more than I meant to. It's so embarrassing to admit, but I might have some leftover casserole, and then some of the cookies I baked, and crackers, and... well, some ice cream, all sort of in one sitting. It's much, much more than I’d ever put on a plate if anyone else were there to see it, of course! \n\nI know it's a silly thing to do at my age. But it's really only after something like that, when I've eaten too much. Does that make sense? I hope you don't think terribly of me for it, I just try to fix the mistake so things stay manageable.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up to undo the calories from the extra food you eat during those times when you feel you've overeaten?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... yes. Yes, I suppose that is exactly it. I feel so terribly guilty about all those extra calories, and I just... I don't want to gain weight after being so foolish. \n\nTo be perfectly honest with you, Doctor—and it is so hard to say this out loud—that's actually what finally scared me into coming in to see you. After I eat too much, I... well, I've been making myself throw up in the bathroom so I won't gain the weight. I kept telling myself I could just do it once in a while to fix a bad evening, but... it’s gotten to where it isn’t just a one-time mistake anymore. \n\nI know it sounds awful for a woman my age to be doing such a thing. You must think I'm very silly, don't you? I just want to keep things under control and do what's right. I hope you can understand... am I making any sense at all?\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up a way for you to feel like you've undone the eating?\n\nPatient: Oh... yes, exactly. I'm sorry, I know I just said that, but... yes. It's... it's just my way of trying to fix it, to undo what I just did. \n\nLike I mentioned, that's really what brought me here today. It's just so frightening to admit, but after I have one of those... those episodes where I eat too much, I go into the bathroom and I make myself sick. I just get so panicked about gaining weight from all that food. \n\nI kept thinking, \"Oh, I'll just do this one time, just to undo this one mistake,\" but... well, it's not just a one-time thing anymore, is it? It's become a bit of a habit, I'm afraid. I know I'm repeating myself, I'm just so nervous talking about it. I hope you aren't too disappointed in me. I really am trying to do the right thing by telling you, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that purging is necessary to maintain a body shape or weight that you consider acceptable?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... well, yes. I suppose it is. I mean, I try to keep myself looking presentable, you know? It's important to take pride in your appearance, isn't it? \n\nBut... well, I do get terribly upset about it sometimes. More than I probably should. If I'm getting dressed for church on Sunday and my skirt feels even a little bit snug... or if I'm at the store and I catch a glimpse of my side view in one of the mirrors... oh, it just ruins my whole day. I start to feel so awful, like... like I've completely let myself go, and I just know everyone else can see it too. \n\nIt's so silly to care so much at 66, I know. A woman my age should be past all this fussing. But I just want to look nice and acceptable, and when I feel heavy, I just... I panic. That's why I end up doing what I do, just to try and keep things where they should be. I hope that doesn't sound too vain or foolish to you? I really do try my best to be sensible most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up only after eating a lot more than usual a way to counteract the calories from that extra food?\n\nPatient: Oh, yes... yes, that's exactly it. I know we keep circling back to this, and I'm so sorry to be repeating myself, but... yes. \n\nThat's the very thing that finally frightened me enough to make an appointment with you. After I have one of those times where I eat far too much, I... I go into the bathroom and make myself throw up. I just get so terribly afraid of putting on weight from all those extra calories. \n\nAt first, I told myself it was just a little trick to fix a mistake, you know? Just something I'd do once in a great while. But... well, it's gotten to the point where it isn't just a one-time mistake anymore. It's happening more often, and I just couldn't ignore it any longer. I'm trying to be completely honest with you, Doctor... I hope I'm giving you the right answers? I just want to do whatever I need to do to get this sorted out.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up regularly as part of your normal routine to control your weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... the word \"routine\" sounds so dreadful, doesn't it? I never, ever wanted it to be a normal part of my life. But... well, if I'm being completely truthful with you—and I really am trying to be a good patient and tell you everything—I suppose it has become exactly that. \n\nFor at least the past several months now, it’s... well, it's been happening most weeks. I'll have one of those awful secret eating spells we talked about, and then I just... I go and make myself sick afterward. I kept telling myself it was just every now and then, but I can’t honestly call it occasional anymore, as much as I wish I could. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that's probably very bad to hear. I hope you aren't too disappointed in me for letting it get to this point? I really am trying my best to fix it by being honest with you today.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the past year where you lost a significant amount of weight, only to regain it later?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no. No, I haven't really had anything like that. My weight has stayed pretty much the same, give or take a pound or two here and there. I haven't had any big drops or anything dramatic like that. \n\nI try to keep things steady, you see. I hope that's a good thing? I mean, I know the... the other things I've told you aren't good at all, but at least my weight hasn't been bouncing all over the place. That's a little bit of a positive, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually make yourself throw up only after eating episodes where you feel like you've lost control?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... I'm actually not entirely sure. I haven't really noticed if it's *only* when I feel like I've completely lost control. \n\nI mean, I know it definitely happens after those... those bigger eating spells we just talked about. But as for whether it's strictly tied to feeling a complete loss of control every single time... I just haven't paid close enough attention to my exact feelings in the moment to say for certain. \n\nI'm so sorry, I feel like I should have a much better answer for you! I hope I'm not being unhelpful or making your job harder. I could certainly start keeping a little notebook to track exactly how I'm feeling right before it happens, if you think that would be a good idea? I really just want to make sure I'm doing this right.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up only after times when you feel like you've eaten without being able to stop?\n\nPatient: Oh, I... I think that's sort of similar to what you just asked, isn't it? About losing control? \n\nI really wish I could give you a perfectly clear \"yes\" or \"no\" on this. I just... I suppose I haven't really separated the feeling of \"losing control\" from the feeling of \"not being able to stop.\" To me, they just feel like... well, like one big, awful blur of a mistake. \n\nI don't mean to be repetitive or frustrating, I promise! I just... I honestly don't know if it's *only* when I feel like I can't stop. Sometimes I think I just feel so terribly guilty about whatever I've eaten, even if it wasn't a huge amount, that I just... I feel like I have to fix it. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm explaining myself properly. I really am trying to give you exactly what you need to know.\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up always in response to eating a large amount of food in a short time?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... no, not always. I mean, it definitely happens after those times we talked about, when I've eaten quite a lot... but sometimes it's not even a large amount of food. \n\nSometimes I'll just have a little something, maybe a piece of cake or... or a few cookies that I know I shouldn't have had, and I just feel so awful about it. I feel like I've ruined everything, and I just need to make it right again. So I'll do it then, too, even if it wasn't a huge amount of food. \n\nI hope that's not too confusing? I know it probably sounds silly to get so upset over a few cookies. I just try so hard to be good, you know? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of answers... I really want to be helpful.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I really hope you don't think I'm just being terribly shallow.\n\nDoctor: Do you often limit the amount or type of food you eat to avoid gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I wouldn't say I strictly limit things, no. I mean, I try to be good, of course. I think we all try to watch what we eat, especially at my age when the metabolism slows down, right? \n\nI might, um... I might skip a dessert or try to have a salad instead of a sandwich for lunch, just to be sensible. But I don't do those extreme diets or starve myself or anything like that. I try to eat normally most of the time. I just... I try to make healthy choices so I don't get too heavy. That's a normal thing to do, isn't it? I hope that's the right way to handle it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is a problem even when others tell you that you look fine?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I'm not really sure I'd say that. To be perfectly honest, people don't usually comment on my weight one way or the other these days. My friends are very sweet, of course, but we just don't really talk about our bodies much at our age. \n\nAnd I certainly wouldn't ever argue with someone if they were kind enough to tell me I looked nice! I'd just say thank you. I don't think I'm... you know, imagining things or being completely unreasonable when I feel a bit heavy. I just want to look presentable for work and for church, really. I hope that sounds like a normal way to look at things? I'd hate for you to think I'm completely obsessed or making a fuss over nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are overweight even when you are at a normal weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... no, I don't think so. I mean, I know what the scale says, and I know what size clothes I wear. I don't think I'm seeing things that aren't there. \n\nIf my doctor tells me my weight is in a healthy range, I believe them. I just... I suppose I just prefer to stay on the lower end of that range, if I can. It just feels a bit more comfortable, you know? I don't think I have a distorted view of myself or anything like that. I just like to look neat and tidy. That's a perfectly reasonable way to feel, don't you think? I certainly wouldn't want to seem irrational about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I haven't really noticed any big changes. It stays pretty much the same, honestly. \n\nI mean, maybe a pound or two here and there depending on the week, just like anyone else, but nothing significant at all. Being on my feet all day at the store helps keep things pretty steady, I think. I just try to maintain a nice, even weight so my clothes fit right. \n\nThat's what you doctors usually prefer, right? No big ups and downs? I hope I'm doing that part right, at least. I really do try to keep it all very manageable.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually make yourself throw up after eating a normal amount of food or only after eating a lot more than usual?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... um... well, it's usually only if I feel I've really overdone things. I certainly wouldn't do that after just a regular, sensible meal. That wouldn't be right at all. \n\nBut... well, sometimes, if I've had a late shift at the store, or maybe after a bridge night... I might come home and just... eat a bit more than I meant to. It's so embarrassing to admit, but I might have some leftover casserole, and then some of the cookies I baked, and crackers, and... well, some ice cream, all sort of in one sitting. It's much, much more than I’d ever put on a plate if anyone else were there to see it, of course! \n\nI know it's a silly thing to do at my age. But it's really only after something like that, when I've eaten too much. Does that make sense? I hope you don't think terribly of me for it, I just try to fix the mistake so things stay manageable.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up to undo the calories from the extra food you eat during those times when you feel you've overeaten?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... yes. Yes, I suppose that is exactly it. I feel so terribly guilty about all those extra calories, and I just... I don't want to gain weight after being so foolish. \n\nTo be perfectly honest with you, Doctor—and it is so hard to say this out loud—that's actually what finally scared me into coming in to see you. After I eat too much, I... well, I've been making myself throw up in the bathroom so I won't gain the weight. I kept telling myself I could just do it once in a while to fix a bad evening, but... it’s gotten to where it isn’t just a one-time mistake anymore. \n\nI know it sounds awful for a woman my age to be doing such a thing. You must think I'm very silly, don't you? I just want to keep things under control and do what's right. I hope you can understand... am I making any sense at all?\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up a way for you to feel like you've undone the eating?\n\nPatient: Oh... yes, exactly. I'm sorry, I know I just said that, but... yes. It's... it's just my way of trying to fix it, to undo what I just did. \n\nLike I mentioned, that's really what brought me here today. It's just so frightening to admit, but after I have one of those... those episodes where I eat too much, I go into the bathroom and I make myself sick. I just get so panicked about gaining weight from all that food. \n\nI kept thinking, \"Oh, I'll just do this one time, just to undo this one mistake,\" but... well, it's not just a one-time thing anymore, is it? It's become a bit of a habit, I'm afraid. I know I'm repeating myself, I'm just so nervous talking about it. I hope you aren't too disappointed in me. I really am trying to do the right thing by telling you, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that purging is necessary to maintain a body shape or weight that you consider acceptable?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... well, yes. I suppose it is. I mean, I try to keep myself looking presentable, you know? It's important to take pride in your appearance, isn't it? \n\nBut... well, I do get terribly upset about it sometimes. More than I probably should. If I'm getting dressed for church on Sunday and my skirt feels even a little bit snug... or if I'm at the store and I catch a glimpse of my side view in one of the mirrors... oh, it just ruins my whole day. I start to feel so awful, like... like I've completely let myself go, and I just know everyone else can see it too. \n\nIt's so silly to care so much at 66, I know. A woman my age should be past all this fussing. But I just want to look nice and acceptable, and when I feel heavy, I just... I panic. That's why I end up doing what I do, just to try and keep things where they should be. I hope that doesn't sound too vain or foolish to you? I really do try my best to be sensible most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up only after eating a lot more than usual a way to counteract the calories from that extra food?\n\nPatient: Oh, yes... yes, that's exactly it. I know we keep circling back to this, and I'm so sorry to be repeating myself, but... yes. \n\nThat's the very thing that finally frightened me enough to make an appointment with you. After I have one of those times where I eat far too much, I... I go into the bathroom and make myself throw up. I just get so terribly afraid of putting on weight from all those extra calories. \n\nAt first, I told myself it was just a little trick to fix a mistake, you know? Just something I'd do once in a great while. But... well, it's gotten to the point where it isn't just a one-time mistake anymore. It's happening more often, and I just couldn't ignore it any longer. I'm trying to be completely honest with you, Doctor... I hope I'm giving you the right answers? I just want to do whatever I need to do to get this sorted out.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up regularly as part of your normal routine to control your weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... the word \"routine\" sounds so dreadful, doesn't it? I never, ever wanted it to be a normal part of my life. But... well, if I'm being completely truthful with you—and I really am trying to be a good patient and tell you everything—I suppose it has become exactly that. \n\nFor at least the past several months now, it’s... well, it's been happening most weeks. I'll have one of those awful secret eating spells we talked about, and then I just... I go and make myself sick afterward. I kept telling myself it was just every now and then, but I can’t honestly call it occasional anymore, as much as I wish I could. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that's probably very bad to hear. I hope you aren't too disappointed in me for letting it get to this point? I really am trying my best to fix it by being honest with you today.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the past year where you lost a significant amount of weight, only to regain it later?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no. No, I haven't really had anything like that. My weight has stayed pretty much the same, give or take a pound or two here and there. I haven't had any big drops or anything dramatic like that. \n\nI try to keep things steady, you see. I hope that's a good thing? But... well, if I'm getting ready for church and my skirt feels even a little bit tight, or... or if I catch a glimpse of my side view in one of the mirrors at the store where I work... it just, oh, it can completely ruin my whole day. \n\nI just get this awful feeling that everyone is looking at me and can tell I've completely let myself go. I know that sounds so foolish for a woman my age to fret over, doesn't it? I really hope you don't think I'm just being terribly shallow.\n\nDoctor: Do you often limit the amount or type of food you eat to avoid gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I wouldn't say I strictly limit things, no. I mean, I try to be good, of course. I think we all try to watch what we eat, especially at my age when the metabolism slows down, right? \n\nI might, um... I might skip a dessert or try to have a salad instead of a sandwich for lunch, just to be sensible. But I don't do those extreme diets or starve myself or anything like that. I try to eat normally most of the time. I just... I try to make healthy choices so I don't get too heavy. That's a normal thing to do, isn't it? I hope that's the right way to handle it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is a problem even when others tell you that you look fine?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I'm not really sure I'd say that. To be perfectly honest, people don't usually comment on my weight one way or the other these days. My friends are very sweet, of course, but we just don't really talk about our bodies much at our age. \n\nAnd I certainly wouldn't ever argue with someone if they were kind enough to tell me I looked nice! I'd just say thank you. I don't think I'm... you know, imagining things or being completely unreasonable when I feel a bit heavy. I just want to look presentable for work and for church, really. I hope that sounds like a normal way to look at things? I'd hate for you to think I'm completely obsessed or making a fuss over nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are overweight even when you are at a normal weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... no, I don't think so. I mean, I know what the scale says, and I know what size clothes I wear. I don't think I'm seeing things that aren't there. \n\nIf my doctor tells me my weight is in a healthy range, I believe them. I just... I suppose I just prefer to stay on the lower end of that range, if I can. It just feels a bit more comfortable, you know? I don't think I have a distorted view of myself or anything like that. I just like to look neat and tidy. That's a perfectly reasonable way to feel, don't you think? I certainly wouldn't want to seem irrational about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I haven't really noticed any big changes. It stays pretty much the same, honestly. \n\nI mean, maybe a pound or two here and there depending on the week, just like anyone else, but nothing significant at all. Being on my feet all day at the store helps keep things pretty steady, I think. I just try to maintain a nice, even weight so my clothes fit right. \n\nThat's what you doctors usually prefer, right? No big ups and downs? I hope I'm doing that part right, at least. I really do try to keep it all very manageable.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually make yourself throw up after eating a normal amount of food or only after eating a lot more than usual?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... um... well, it's usually only if I feel I've really overdone things. I certainly wouldn't do that after just a regular, sensible meal. That wouldn't be right at all. \n\nBut... well, sometimes, if I've had a late shift at the store, or maybe after a bridge night... I might come home and just... eat a bit more than I meant to. It's so embarrassing to admit, but I might have some leftover casserole, and then some of the cookies I baked, and crackers, and... well, some ice cream, all sort of in one sitting. It's much, much more than I’d ever put on a plate if anyone else were there to see it, of course! \n\nI know it's a silly thing to do at my age. But it's really only after something like that, when I've eaten too much. Does that make sense? I hope you don't think terribly of me for it, I just try to fix the mistake so things stay manageable.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up to undo the calories from the extra food you eat during those times when you feel you've overeaten?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... yes. Yes, I suppose that is exactly it. I feel so terribly guilty about all those extra calories, and I just... I don't want to gain weight after being so foolish. \n\nTo be perfectly honest with you, Doctor—and it is so hard to say this out loud—that's actually what finally scared me into coming in to see you. After I eat too much, I... well, I've been making myself throw up in the bathroom so I won't gain the weight. I kept telling myself I could just do it once in a while to fix a bad evening, but... it’s gotten to where it isn’t just a one-time mistake anymore. \n\nI know it sounds awful for a woman my age to be doing such a thing. You must think I'm very silly, don't you? I just want to keep things under control and do what's right. I hope you can understand... am I making any sense at all?\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up a way for you to feel like you've undone the eating?\n\nPatient: Oh... yes, exactly. I'm sorry, I know I just said that, but... yes. It's... it's just my way of trying to fix it, to undo what I just did. \n\nLike I mentioned, that's really what brought me here today. It's just so frightening to admit, but after I have one of those... those episodes where I eat too much, I go into the bathroom and I make myself sick. I just get so panicked about gaining weight from all that food. \n\nI kept thinking, \"Oh, I'll just do this one time, just to undo this one mistake,\" but... well, it's not just a one-time thing anymore, is it? It's become a bit of a habit, I'm afraid. I know I'm repeating myself, I'm just so nervous talking about it. I hope you aren't too disappointed in me. I really am trying to do the right thing by telling you, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that purging is necessary to maintain a body shape or weight that you consider acceptable?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... well, yes. I suppose it is. I mean, I try to keep myself looking presentable, you know? It's important to take pride in your appearance, isn't it? \n\nBut... well, I do get terribly upset about it sometimes. More than I probably should. If I'm getting dressed for church on Sunday and my skirt feels even a little bit snug... or if I'm at the store and I catch a glimpse of my side view in one of the mirrors... oh, it just ruins my whole day. I start to feel so awful, like... like I've completely let myself go, and I just know everyone else can see it too. \n\nIt's so silly to care so much at 66, I know. A woman my age should be past all this fussing. But I just want to look nice and acceptable, and when I feel heavy, I just... I panic. That's why I end up doing what I do, just to try and keep things where they should be. I hope that doesn't sound too vain or foolish to you? I really do try my best to be sensible most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up only after eating a lot more than usual a way to counteract the calories from that extra food?\n\nPatient: Oh, yes... yes, that's exactly it. I know we keep circling back to this, and I'm so sorry to be repeating myself, but... yes. \n\nThat's the very thing that finally frightened me enough to make an appointment with you. After I have one of those times where I eat far too much, I... I go into the bathroom and make myself throw up. I just get so terribly afraid of putting on weight from all those extra calories. \n\nAt first, I told myself it was just a little trick to fix a mistake, you know? Just something I'd do once in a great while. But... well, it's gotten to the point where it isn't just a one-time mistake anymore. It's happening more often, and I just couldn't ignore it any longer. I'm trying to be completely honest with you, Doctor... I hope I'm giving you the right answers? I just want to do whatever I need to do to get this sorted out.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up regularly as part of your normal routine to control your weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... the word \"routine\" sounds so dreadful, doesn't it? I never, ever wanted it to be a normal part of my life. But... well, if I'm being completely truthful with you—and I really am trying to be a good patient and tell you everything—I suppose it has become exactly that. \n\nFor at least the past several months now, it’s... well, it's been happening most weeks. I'll have one of those awful secret eating spells we talked about, and then I just... I go and make myself sick afterward. I kept telling myself it was just every now and then, but I can’t honestly call it occasional anymore, as much as I wish I could. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that's probably very bad to hear. I hope you aren't too disappointed in me for letting it get to this point? I really am trying my best to fix it by being honest with you today.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the past year where you lost a significant amount of weight, only to regain it later?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no. No, I haven't really had anything like that. My weight has stayed pretty much the same, give or take a pound or two here and there. I haven't had any big drops or anything dramatic like that. \n\nI try to keep things steady, you see. I hope that's a good thing? I mean, I know the... the other things I've told you aren't good at all, but at least my weight hasn't been bouncing all over the place. That's a little bit of a positive, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually make yourself throw up only after eating episodes where you feel like you've lost control?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... I'm actually not entirely sure. I haven't really noticed if it's *only* when I feel like I've completely lost control. \n\nI mean, I know it definitely happens after those... those bigger eating spells we just talked about. But as for whether it's strictly tied to feeling a complete loss of control every single time... I just haven't paid close enough attention to my exact feelings in the moment to say for certain. \n\nI'm so sorry, I feel like I should have a much better answer for you! I hope I'm not being unhelpful or making your job harder. I could certainly start keeping a little notebook to track exactly how I'm feeling right before it happens, if you think that would be a good idea? I really just want to make sure I'm doing this right.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up only after times when you feel like you've eaten without being able to stop?\n\nPatient: Oh, I... I think that's sort of similar to what you just asked, isn't it? About losing control? \n\nI really wish I could give you a perfectly clear \"yes\" or \"no\" on this. I just... I suppose I haven't really separated the feeling of \"losing control\" from the feeling of \"not being able to stop.\" To me, they just feel like... well, like one big, awful blur of a mistake. \n\nI don't mean to be repetitive or frustrating, I promise! I just... I honestly don't know if it's *only* when I feel like I can't stop. Sometimes I think I just feel so terribly guilty about whatever I've eaten, even if it wasn't a huge amount, that I just... I feel like I have to fix it. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm explaining myself properly. I really am trying to give you exactly what you need to know.\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up always in response to eating a large amount of food in a short time?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... no, not always. I mean, it definitely happens after those times we talked about, when I've eaten quite a lot... but sometimes it's not even a large amount of food. \n\nSometimes I'll just have a little something, maybe a piece of cake or... or a few cookies that I know I shouldn't have had, and I just feel so awful about it. I feel like I've ruined everything, and I just need to make it right again. So I'll do it then, too, even if it wasn't a huge amount of food. \n\nI hope that's not too confusing? I know it probably sounds silly to get so upset over a few cookies. I just try so hard to be good, you know? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of answers... I really want to be helpful.\n\nDoctor: Is your decision to make yourself throw up more about fearing weight gain or feeling guilty about what you've eaten?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... I'm really not sure how to answer that one. I hope it's alright if I say I just don't know? \n\nIt's... well, they both sort of swirl together in my head. I feel terribly guilty, yes, but I also worry so much about gaining weight. But as for which one is the main reason I actually... you know, go through with it... I just haven't been able to untangle that. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know you're trying to get to the bottom of this and I'm probably not being very clear. It's just all a bit jumbled for me. Am I doing okay? I can try to sit and think much harder about it if you really need me to pick one. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Is your decision to make yourself throw up usually because you're afraid of gaining weight from what you've eaten?\n\nPatient: Oh... yes. Yes, I suppose that really is it. I just get so terribly frightened of putting on weight after I've eaten too much. \n\nActually, that's... well, to be perfectly honest, that's what finally scared me into coming in to see you. I kept trying to convince myself it was just a silly slip-up, you know? But I've been going into the bathroom and making myself throw up so I won't gain the weight, and... it's just gotten to where I can't pretend it's a one-time mistake anymore. \n\nI feel so embarrassed admitting that out loud, especially at my age. I hope you don't think too badly of me? I know it's not a proper thing to do, I just panic so much about the weight. I really am trying to be completely open with you so you have what you need. Am I doing alright explaining it?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
